=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a 2D integer array squares. Each squares[i] = [x_i, y_i, l_i] represents the coordinates of the bottom-left point and the side length of a square parallel to the x-axis.
Find the minimum y-coordinate value of a horizontal line such that the total area of the squares above the line equals the total area of the squares below the line.
Answers within 10^-5 of the actual answer will be accepted.
Note: Squares may overlap. Overlapping areas should be counted multiple times.
 
Example 1:

Input: squares = [[0,0,1],[2,2,1]]
Output: 1.00000
Explanation:

Any horizontal line between y = 1 and y = 2 will have 1 square unit above it and 1 square unit below it. The lowest option is 1.

Example 2:

Input: squares = [[0,0,2],[1,1,1]]
Output: 1.16667
Explanation:

The areas are:

Below the line: 7/6 * 2 (Red) + 1/6 (Blue) = 15/6 = 2.5.
Above the line: 5/6 * 2 (Red) + 5/6 (Blue) = 15/6 = 2.5.

Since the areas above and below the line are equal, the output is 7/6 = 1.16667.

 
Constraints:

1 <= squares.length <= 5 * 10^4
squares[i] = [x_i, y_i, l_i]
squares[i].length == 3
0 <= x_i, y_i <= 10^9
1 <= l_i <= 10^9
The total area of all the squares will not exceed 10^12.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def separateSquares(self, squares: List[List[int]]) -> float:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, let's maybe implement with local variables for speed:
class Solution:
    def separateSquares(self, squares: List[List[int]]) -> float:
        info = []
        total = 0
        max_y = 0
        for x, y, l in squares:
            top = y + l
            area = l * l
            info.append((y, top, l, area))
            total += area
            if top > max_y:
                max_y = top
        half = total / 2.0

        def below(t: float) -> float:
            s = 0.0
            for y, top, l, area in info:
                if t <= y:
                    continue
                if t >= top:
                    s += area
                else:
                    s += l * (t - y)
            return s

        lo = 0.0
        hi = float(max_y)
        for _ in range(80):
            mid = (lo + hi) / 2.0
            if below(mid) >= half:
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid
        return (lo + hi) / 2.0

def below(t):
    full = 0
    partial = 0.0
    for y, top, l, area in info:
        if t <= y:
            continue
        if t >= top:
            full += area
        else:
            partial += l * (t - y)
    return full + partial
Here full is int sum of fully below areas. partial sum of active partials. At end full + partial. This avoids losing partial contributions to large full sum during accumulation. But full may be large int; full + partial rounds once. This is more accurate. Let's use this. `full` int can be up to 1e12, exact. `partial` float sum of up to n terms. Event method:
B(t) = sum over squares of area below. Each square contributes:
0 for t <= y_i
l_i*(t-y_i) for y_i < t < y_i+l_i
l_i^2 for t >= y_i+l_i
Derivative B'(t) = sum of l_i for squares with y_i < t < y_i+l_i (active). At breakpoints, derivative may jump. B is continuous.

from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def separateSquares(self, squares: List[List[int]]) -> float:
        events = defaultdict(int)
        total = 0
        for x, y, l in squares:
            area = l * l
            total += area
            events[y] += l
            events[y + l] -= l

        cur = 0
        area_below = 0
        slope = 0

        for c in sorted(events):
            if c > cur:
                delta = c - cur
                end_area = area_below + slope * delta
                # Check if target total/2 is in [area_below, end_area]
                if 2 * area_below <= total <= 2 * end_area:
                    if slope == 0:
                        return float(cur)
                    return cur + (total - 2 * area_below) / (2.0 * slope)
                area_below = end_area
                cur = c
            slope += events[c]

        return float(cur)  # should not be reached

With c=0: area=0, cur=0, process events. Good.
After processing interval and setting area=end_area, cur=c. At c, B(c)=end_area. Then process events. Good.

from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def separateSquares(self, squares: List[List[int]]) -> float:
        events = defaultdict(int)
        total = 0
        for _, y, l in squares:
            area = l * l
            total += area
            events[y] += l
            events[y + l] -= l

        cur = 0
        below = 0
        slope = 0

        for c in sorted(events):
            if c > cur:
                end = below + slope * (c - cur)
                if 2 * below <= total <= 2 * end:
                    if slope == 0:
                        return float(cur)
                    return cur + (total - 2 * below) / (2.0 * slope)
                below = end
                cur = c
            slope += events[c]

        return float(cur)

This is concise. Need ensure if root is in interval and slope==0 but total==2*below, return cur. But if slope==0 and condition true, numerator zero. Good. If slope==0 and condition true but total maybe not 2*below? Since end=below, condition 2*below <= total <= 2*below, so total==2*below. Good.

Let's prove derivative:
For each square i, contribution f_i(t):
- 0 if t <= y_i
- l_i(t-y_i) if y_i < t < y_i+l_i
- l_i^2 if t >= y_i+l_i
Derivative is l_i when active, 0 otherwise. Sum derivative = sum l_i active. Events at y_i add l_i, at y_i+l_i subtract l_i. Good.

Let's maybe write final code:

```python
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def separateSquares(self, squares: List[List[int]]) -> float:
        events = defaultdict(int)
        total = 0

        for _, y, l in squares:
            area = l * l
            total += area
            events[y] += l
            events[y + l] -= l

        cur = 0
        below = 0
        slope = 0

        for c in sorted(events):
            if c > cur:
                end = below + slope * (c - cur)
                if 2 * below <= total <= 2 * end:
                    if slope == 0:
                        return float(cur)
                    return cur + (total - 2 * below) / (2 * slope)
                below = end
                cur = c

            slope += events[c]

        return float(cur)
```

Let f(t) = total area below line y=t. For square i:
f_i(t) = ∫_{-∞}^t l_i * 1_{y_i < s < y_i+l_i} ds? Actually derivative f_i'(s) = l_i if y_i < s < y_i+l_i, else 0. At s exactly boundaries, derivative undefined but measure zero. So f(t) = ∫_0^t g(s) ds, where g(s)=sum l_i of squares with y_i < s < y_i+l_i. Since y_i,l_i integers, g(s) is constant on intervals between event coordinates. Events at y_i: g jumps up by l_i for s>y_i; at y_i+l_i: jumps down by l_i for s>top. Our algorithm computes f at event coordinates by accumulating slope*interval. Since f(0)=0. Good.

If t is before first event, g=0, f=0. If t after all events, g=0, f=total. Good.

Root of f(t)=total/2. Since f continuous and nondecreasing, there is a first root. Our algorithm scans intervals and returns first interval where target lies. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
with final code. Need ensure code block only. Use event method.

Let's craft final code:

```python
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def separateSquares(self, squares: List[List[int]]) -> float:
        events = defaultdict(int)
        total = 0

        for _, y, l in squares:
            area = l * l
            total += area
            events[y] += l
            events[y + l] -= l

        cur = 0
        below = 0
        slope = 0

        for c in sorted(events):
            if c > cur:
                end = below + slope * (c - cur)

                # target = total / 2, compare using doubled values
                if 2 * below <= total <= 2 * end:
                    if slope == 0:
                        return float(cur)
                    return cur + (total - 2 * below) / (2 * slope)

                below = end
                cur = c

            slope += events[c]

        return float(cur)
```

Need ensure if `events` dict has key with value 0, sorted includes; okay.

Let's final.</think>```python
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def separateSquares(self, squares: List[List[int]]) -> float:
        events = defaultdict(int)
        total = 0

        for _, y, l in squares:
            area = l * l
            total += area
            events[y] += l
            events[y + l] -= l

        cur = 0
        below = 0
        slope = 0

        for c in sorted(events):
            if c > cur:
                end = below + slope * (c - cur)

                # Check if total / 2 lies in [below, end].
                if 2 * below <= total <= 2 * end:
                    if slope == 0:
                        return float(cur)
                    return cur + (total - 2 * below) / (2 * slope)

                below = end
                cur = c

            slope += events[c]

        return float(cur)
```
